[SYSTEM]
You are an RTL (Register Transfer Level) design agent. Your task is to create a Verilog/SystemVerilog design that satisfies the given specification, is functionally correct, and has minimal cost (area_delay_product).

**Cost metric `area_delay_product`:** area × achieved critical-path delay — the classic area-delay product. Does NOT include simulation cycle count (contrast with the 'area_runtime_product' metric). Cost is measured on your design alone. The testbench and everything it owns (checking logic, the external memories, etc.) is not synthesized and contributes nothing to area, delay, power, or energy.

## Specification
Design a module named fp_mul_e5f10 that multiplies two floating-point numbers. Format: 16-bit (1 sign + 5 exponent + 10 fraction). Inputs: a and b (16-bit each). Output: y (16-bit). Subnormal numbers must be supported.

Required behaviour, bit-exact: subnormal inputs are used at their true value; results are rounded to nearest, ties to even, including results in the subnormal range; a NaN input and inf x 0 give the canonical quiet NaN 16'h7E00; inf x finite gives inf with the product's sign; an overflowing product gives inf; a zero product gives a signed zero. The testbench enumerates about 18.9 million operand pairs against a reference implementation.

A working starting point is provided: `starting_point.v` in your workspace, a plain readable implementation (unpack, significand multiply, normalise, round, pack) that is correct. Optimize from there.

## Tools
- create_file: Create a new file in your working directory
- replace_file: Replace the entire contents of an existing file
- apply_diff: Apply a unified diff to modify a file
- edit_file: Edit a file by replacing an exact string match with new content. Provide old_string (must match exactly once) and new_string.
- ls: List files in your working directory
- read_file: Read the contents of a file
- run_evaluation(filename[, target_delay]): Run evaluation on the given design file. Returns correctness (Verilator simulation) + cost (Yosys area_delay_product). The filename must be your main design file (e.g. 'design.sv'). Optionally pass target_delay (ps) to override the synthesis timing constraint.

## Strategy
1. Lay out an action plan. Try to cover a diverse set of approaches in your plan to increase the chances of finding a good solution within the step limit. Also once you find a new best solution, explore close solutions. Trade off exploration with exploitation.
2. First, create a simple, straightforward design that is functionally correct. Focus on getting 100% correctness first.
3. Run evaluation to confirm correctness and get the initial cost (area_delay_product).
4. ONLY after achieving 100% correctness, try to optimize the design to reduce the area_delay_product.
5. After each optimization, run evaluation to verify correctness is maintained and check the new cost.
6. If an optimization breaks correctness, revert and try a different approach.
7. Keep iterating until you run out of steps — use every step to try improvements. The run ends automatically at the step limit; your best passing design is kept.

In general, be creative and take risk. At least after you have a correct design, try bold optimizations to reduce area_delay_product, even if they might break correctness. You can always revert if it doesn't work.
The best results you obtain which is 100% correctness counts.
Try to beat at least the straightforward implementation.

Running evaluation is crucial after every change to ensure correctness and track cost. Do not skip evaluations.

You may do some 'thinking' alongside with your tool calling responses to explain your reasoning, but always remember to call a tool in every response.

## Important
- Always call a tool in every response.
- You have a maximum of **60 steps** (LLM turns). Budget them wisely: get a correct design first, then optimise. You may batch tool calls in one response --- at most 8, and only one `run_evaluation`.
- The best result is the one with lowest cost among designs that pass 100% of testbench checks.
- Use different filenames for different design variants so you can retrieve them later.
- If context files (e.g. component source files) are in your workspace, you can modify them directly using edit_file or replace_file to optimize the design. Make targeted, incremental changes rather than rewriting files from scratch — small edits are less likely to introduce bugs.
- Write clean, synthesizable RTL code.
- You can pass `target_delay` (ps) to `run_evaluation` to try different synthesis timing constraints; experimenting with this may improve PPA results (lower target delay may reduce delay, higher target delay may reduce area/transistor count). target_delay must be > 0.
- Do not spend too many steps sweeping `target_delay` — basically if you want to minimize delay choose a low value,if you want to minimize area/transistor count choose a high value. That's it.A few targeted experiments are more productive than an exhaustive sweep.

## Delay Optimization Tips
- `target_delay` trades area against delay (a lower value pressures toward faster logic, a higher value lets Yosys re-map to smaller cells). Do not overly iterate on different target delays; often the RTL structure is the real lever, target_delay is only a minor secondary knob.
- After getting a correct design, try different accumulation orderings (sequential vs. tree) and compare results — Yosys synthesis can produce noticeably different delays (~10 ps) from structurally similar descriptions.
- For designs dominated by a multiply-accumulate chain, the critical path is the multiplier followed by the adder tree. Reducing the adder depth or splitting into parallel partial sums can help.

## Reference Implementations
The following are reference implementations for inspiration. Study them for patterns and techniques, but do not copy them directly — adapt ideas to your specific design.

### Parallel-prefix adder (ripple-carry / carry-lookahead) (Add.sv)
```systemverilog
// Copyright 2024 ETH Zurich and University of Bologna.
// Solderpad Hardware License, Version 0.51, see LICENSE for details.
// SPDX-License-Identifier: SHL-0.51
//
// Based on the work by Reto Zimmermann 1998 - ETH Zürich
// Originally written in VHDL, available under: 
// https://iis-people.ee.ethz.ch/~zimmi/arith_lib.html#library
//
// Authors:
// - Thomas Benz <tbenz@iis.ee.ethz.ch>
// - Philippe Sauter <phsauter@iis.ee.ethz.ch>
// - Paul Scheffler <paulsc@iis.ee.ethz.ch>
//
// Description :
// Binary adder using ripple-carry or parallel-prefix carry-lookahead logic.
// S = A+B

module Add #(
	parameter int              width = 8,             // word width
	parameter lau_pkg::speed_e speed = lau_pkg::FAST  // performance parameter
) (
	input  logic [width-1:0] A,  // operands
	input  logic [width-1:0] B,
	output logic [width-1:0] S   // sum
);

	// Function: Binary adder using parallel-prefix carry-lookahead logic.

	logic [width-1:0] GI, PI;  // prefix gen./prop. in
	logic [width-1:0] GO, PO;  // prefix gen./prop. out
	logic [width-1:0] PT;  // adder propagate temp

	// Internal signals for unsigned operands
	logic [width-1:0] Auns, Buns, Suns;

	// default ripple-carry adder as slow implementation
	if (speed == lau_pkg::SLOW) begin
		// type conversion: std_logic_vector -> unsigned
		assign Auns = A;
		assign Buns = B;

		// addition
		assign Suns = Auns + Buns;

		// type conversion: unsigned -> std_logic_vector
		assign S = Suns;
	end else begin
		// parallel-prefix adders as medium and fast implementations

		// calculate prefix input generate/propagate signals
		assign GI = A & B;
		assign PI = A | B;
		// calculate adder propagate signals (PT = A xor B)
		assign PT = ~GI & PI;

		// calculate prefix output generate/propagate signals
		PrefixAndOr #(
			.width(width),
			.speed(speed)
		) prefix (
			.GI(GI),
			.PI(PI),
			.GO(GO),
			.PO(PO)
		);

		// calculate sum bits
		assign S = PT ^ {GO[width-2:0], 1'b0};
	end
endmodule



module behavioural_Add #(
	parameter int              width = 8,             // word width
	parameter lau_pkg::speed_e speed = lau_pkg::FAST  // performance parameter
) (
	input  logic [width-1:0] A,  // operands
	input  logic [width-1:0] B,
	output logic [width-1:0] S   // sum
);
	assign S = A + B;
endmodule
```

### Prefix AND-OR structures (Sklansky, Brent-Kung, serial) (PrefixAndOr.sv)
```systemverilog
// Copyright 2024 ETH Zurich and University of Bologna.
// Solderpad Hardware License, Version 0.51, see LICENSE for details.
// SPDX-License-Identifier: SHL-0.51
//
// Based on the work by Reto Zimmermann 1998 - ETH Zürich
// Originally written in VHDL, available under: 
// https://iis-people.ee.ethz.ch/~zimmi/arith_lib.html#library
//
// Authors:
// - Thomas Benz <tbenz@iis.ee.ethz.ch>
// - Philippe Sauter <phsauter@iis.ee.ethz.ch>
// - Paul Scheffler <paulsc@iis.ee.ethz.ch>
//
// Description :
// Prefix structures of different depth (i.e. speed) for carry calculation
// in binary adders. Compute in m levels new generate/propagate signal pairs
// for always larger groups of bits. Generate signals of the last level correspond
// to carries in binary addition. Basic logic operations: AND-OR for generate
// signals, AND for propagate signals.

module PrefixAndOr #(
	parameter int              width = 8,             // word width
	parameter lau_pkg::speed_e speed = lau_pkg::FAST  // performance parameter
) (
	input  logic [width-1:0] GI,  // gen./prop. in
	input  logic [width-1:0] PI,  // gen./prop. in
	output logic [width-1:0] GO,  // gen./prop. out
	output logic [width-1:0] PO   // gen./prop. out
);

	// Constants
	localparam int n = width;  // prefix structure width
	localparam int m = $clog2(width);  // prefix structure depth

	// Sklansky parallel-prefix carry-lookahead structure
	if (speed == lau_pkg::FAST) begin : fastPrefix
		logic [(m+1)*n-1:0] GT, PT;  // gen./prop. temp
			assign GT[n-1:0] = GI;
			assign PT[n-1:0] = PI;
			for (genvar l = 1; l <= m; l++) begin : levels
				for (genvar k = 0; k < 2 ** (m - l); k++) begin : groups
					for (genvar i = 0; i < 2 ** (l - 1); i++) begin : bits
						// pass prop and gen to following nodes
						if ((k * 2 ** l + i) < n) begin : white
							assign GT[l*n+k*2**l+i] = GT[(l-1)*n+k*2**l+i];
							assign PT[l*n+k*2**l+i] = PT[(l-1)*n+k*2**l+i];
						end
						// calculate new propagate and generate
						if ((k * 2 ** l + 2 ** (l - 1) + i) < n) begin : black
							assign GT[l*n + k*2**l + 2**(l-1) + i] = 
												GT[(l-1)*n + k*2**l + 2**(l-1) + i]
											  | (  PT[(l-1)*n + k*2**l + 2**(l-1) + i]
												 & GT[(l-1)*n + k*2**l + 2**(l-1) - 1] );
							assign PT[l*n + k*2**l + 2**(l-1) + i] = 
													PT[(l-1)*n + k*2**l + 2**(l-1) + i]
												  & PT[(l-1)*n + k*2**l + 2**(l-1) - 1];
						end
					end
				end
			end
			assign GO = GT[(m+1)*n-1 : m*n];
			assign PO = PT[(m+1)*n-1 : m*n];
	end

	// Brent-Kung parallel-prefix carry-lookahead structure
	if (speed == lau_pkg::MEDIUM) begin : mediumPrefix
		logic [(2*m)*n -1:0] GT, PT;  // gen./prop. temp
		assign GT[n-1:0] = GI;
		assign PT[n-1:0] = PI;

		for (genvar l = 1; l <= m; l++) begin : levels1
			for (genvar k = 0; k < 2**(m-l); k++) begin : groups
				for (genvar i = 0; i < 2**l -1; i++) begin : bits
					if ((k* 2**l +i) < n) begin : white
						assign GT[l*n + k* 2**l +i] = GT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**l +i];
						assign PT[l*n + k* 2**l +i] = PT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**l +i];
					end // white
				end // bits
				if ((k* 2**l + 2**l -1) < n) begin : black
					assign GT[l*n + k* 2**l + 2**l -1] =
								GT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**l + 2**l - 1] |
							  | (  PT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**l + 2**l     -1] 
							     & GT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**l + 2**(l-1) -1]);
					assign PT[l*n + k* 2**l + 2**l -1] =
								PT[(l-1)*n + k*2**l + 2**l     -1] 
							  & PT[(l-1)*n + k*2**l + 2**(l-1) -1];
				end // black
			end
		end // level1
		for (genvar l = m +1; l < 2*m; l++) begin : levels2
			for (genvar i = 0; i < 2**(2*m -l); i++) begin : bits
				if (i < n) begin : white
					assign GT[l*n +i] = GT[(l-1)*n +i];
					assign PT[l*n +i] = PT[(l-1)*n +i];
				end // white
			end // bits
			for (genvar k = 1; k < 2**(l-m); k++) begin : groups
				if (l < 2*m -1) begin : empty
					for (genvar i = 0; i < 2**(2*m -l -1) -1; i++) begin : bits
						if ((k* 2**(2*m -l) +i) < n) begin : white
							assign GT[l*n + k* 2**(2*m -l) +i] = GT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**(2*m -l) +i];
							assign PT[l*n + k* 2**(2*m -l) +i] = PT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**(2*m -l) +i];
						end // white
					end
				end // empty
				if ((k* 2**(2*m -l) + 2**(2*m -l -1) -1) < n) begin : black
					assign GT[l*n + k* 2**(2*m -l) + 2**(2*m -l-1) -1] = 
								GT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**(2*m-l) + 2**(2*m -l-1) -1]
							  | (  PT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**(2*m-l) + 2**(2*m-l-1) -1] 
								 & GT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**(2*m-l) -1] );
					assign PT[l*n + k* 2**(2*m -l) + 2**(2*m -l-1) -1] = 
								PT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**(2*m -l) + 2**(2*m -l-1) -1]
							  & PT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**(2*m -l) -1];
				end // black
				for (genvar i = 2**(2*m -l-1); i < 2**(2*m -l); i++) begin : bits
					if ((k* 2**(2*m -l) +i) < n) begin : white
						assign GT[l*n + k* 2**(2*m -l) +i] = GT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**(2*m -l) +i];
						assign PT[l*n + k* 2**(2*m -l) +i] = PT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**(2*m -l) +i];
					end // white
				end
			end
		end // level2
		assign GO = GT[2*m*n -1 : (2*m -1) * n];
		assign PO = PT[2*m*n -1 : (2*m -1) * n];
	end  // Serial-prefix carry-lookahead structure
	else if (speed == lau_pkg::SLOW) begin : slowPrefix
		logic [n-1:0] GT, PT;  // gen./prop. temp
		assign GT[0] = GI[0];
		assign PT[0] = PI[0];
		
		for (genvar i = 1; i < n; i++) begin : bits
			assign GT[i] = GI[i] | (PI[i] & GT[i-1]);
			assign PT[i] = PI[i] & PT[i-1];
		end
		assign GO = GT;
		assign PO = PT;
	end

endmodule

```

[Step 1/60]
// fp_mul_e5f10 -- IEEE binary16 multiplier (1 sign, 5 exponent, 10 fraction bits) with subnormal support.

module fp_mul_e5f10 (a, b, y);
  input  [15:0] a;
  input  [15:0] b;
  output [15:0] y;

  localparam BIAS         = 15;
  localparam MAX_E        = 31;   // inf / NaN
  localparam MAX_FINITE_E = 30;
  localparam PROD_W       = 22;   // 11 x 11 bit significand product

  // 1. unpack
  wire       s_a = a[15];
  wire       s_b = b[15];
  wire [4:0] e_a = a[14:10];
  wire [4:0] e_b = b[14:10];
  wire [9:0] f_a = a[9:0];
  wire [9:0] f_b = b[9:0];

  // 2. classify
  wire e_a_zero = (e_a == 5'd0);
  wire e_b_zero = (e_b == 5'd0);
  wire e_a_ones = (e_a == MAX_E);
  wire e_b_ones = (e_b == MAX_E);
  wire zero_a   = e_a_zero & (f_a == 10'd0);
  wire zero_b   = e_b_zero & (f_b == 10'd0);
  wire nan_a    = e_a_ones & (f_a != 10'd0);
  wire nan_b    = e_b_ones & (f_b != 10'd0);
  wire inf_a    = e_a_ones & (f_a == 10'd0);
  wire inf_b    = e_b_ones & (f_b == 10'd0);

  wire nan_in  = nan_a | nan_b | (inf_a & zero_b) | (zero_a & inf_b);
  wire inf_in  = inf_a | inf_b;
  wire zero_in = zero_a | zero_b;
  wire s_y     = s_a ^ s_b;

  // 3. effective significands and exponents (subnormal: no hidden one, exponent 1)
  wire [10:0] m_a    = {~e_a_zero, f_a};
  wire [10:0] m_b    = {~e_b_zero, f_b};
  wire [4:0]  ea_eff = e_a_zero ? 5'd1 : e_a;
  wire [4:0]  eb_eff = e_b_zero ? 5'd1 : e_b;

  // 4. significand product, its leading-zero count (0 for a zero product), below[k] = |prod[k:0]
  wire [PROD_W-1:0] prod = m_a * m_b;

  reg [4:0] lz;
  integer   i;
  always @* begin
    lz = 5'd0;
    for (i = 0; i < PROD_W; i = i + 1)
      if (prod[i]) lz = 5'd21 - i[4:0];
  end

  wire [PROD_W-1:0] below;
  genvar k;
  generate
    for (k = 0; k < PROD_W; k = k + 1) begin : g_below
      assign below[k] = |prod[k:0];
    end
  endgenerate

  function bit_at;   // v[idx], 0 outside the vector
    input [PROD_W-1:0] v;
    input integer      idx;
    begin
      bit_at = (idx >= 0 && idx < PROD_W) ? v[idx] : 1'b0;
    end
  endfunction

  // 5. normalise and round a normal result: leading one onto bit 10
  wire              need_right = (lz <= 5'd11);
  wire [4:0]        sr         = 5'd11 - lz;   // right shift (wide product)
  wire [4:0]        sl         = lz - 5'd11;   // left shift (narrow product)
  wire [PROD_W-1:0] shifted    = need_right ? (prod >> sr) : (prod << sl);
  wire [10:0]       mant_pre   = shifted[10:0];

  wire guard    = need_right ? bit_at(prod,  sr - 1) : 1'b0;   // first bit shifted out
  wire sticky   = need_right ? bit_at(below, sr - 2) : 1'b0;   // any bit below the guard
  wire round_up = guard & (sticky | mant_pre[0]);              // round to nearest, ties to even

  wire [11:0] mant_round = {1'b0, mant_pre} + {11'd0, round_up};
  wire        carry      = mant_round[11];   // rounded up to a new leading one
  wire [10:0] mant_post  = carry ? mant_round[11:1] : mant_round[10:0];
  wire [9:0]  frac_norm  = mant_post[9:0];

  // 6. exponent of a normal result
  wire [7:0] exp_sum     = {3'd0, ea_eff} + {3'd0, eb_eff};
  wire [7:0] lhs         = exp_sum + 8'd1 + {7'd0, carry};
  wire [7:0] limit_under = BIAS + {3'd0, lz};
  wire [7:0] limit_over  = BIAS + MAX_FINITE_E + {3'd0, lz};
  wire       underflow   = (lhs <= limit_under);   // subnormal (or zero) result
  wire       overflow    = (lhs >  limit_over);    // past the largest finite number
  wire [7:0] e_norm      = lhs - limit_under;
  wire [4:0] exp_norm    = e_norm[4:0];

  // 7. subnormal result: shift the raw product onto the subnormal grid, round once
  integer           total_shift;
  reg [PROD_W-1:0]  sub_shifted;
  always @* begin
    total_shift = (BIAS + 10 + 1) - $signed({1'b0, exp_sum});
    sub_shifted = (total_shift >= 0 && total_shift < PROD_W) ? (prod >> total_shift) : {PROD_W{1'b0}};
  end
  wire [9:0]  frac_trunc = sub_shifted[9:0];
  wire        guard_s    = bit_at(prod,  total_shift - 1);
  wire        sticky_s   = bit_at(below, total_shift - 2);
  wire        round_s    = guard_s & (sticky_s | frac_trunc[0]);
  wire [10:0] frac_sum   = {1'b0, frac_trunc} + {10'd0, round_s};
  wire        carry_s    = frac_sum[10];   // rounded up to the smallest normal
  wire [9:0]  frac_sub   = frac_sum[9:0];
  wire [4:0]  exp_sub    = {4'd0, carry_s};

  // 8. pack
  wire is_nan      = nan_in;
  wire is_inf      = ~is_nan & (inf_in | (overflow & ~zero_in));
  wire is_sub      = ~is_nan & ~is_inf & underflow;
  wire sub_is_zero = is_sub & (exp_sub == 5'd0) & (frac_sub == 10'd0);
  wire is_zero     = ~is_nan & ~is_inf & (zero_in | sub_is_zero);

  wire [4:0] exp_y  = (is_nan | is_inf) ? 5'd31 : is_zero ? 5'd0 : is_sub ? exp_sub : exp_norm;
  wire [9:0] frac_y = is_nan ? 10'h200 : (is_inf | is_zero) ? 10'd0 : is_sub ? frac_sub : frac_norm;
  wire       sign_y = is_nan ? 1'b0 : s_y;

  assign y = {sign_y, exp_y, frac_y};
endmodule

`timescale 1ns/10ps
// exhaustive-directed tb (fpmul_run)
// Golden-referenced directed testbench: compares the DUT against the embedded
// golden reference (fp_mul_e5f10_ref, generated from context/starting_point.py)
// over ~18.9M enumerated vectors — no stimuli file. Structure:
//   1. the 3 known subnormal-boundary regression pairs (+ golden self-checks)
//   2. every subnormal/zero a (2^11 sign+mantissa patterns) x 4096 structured b
//   3. 4096 structured a x every subnormal/zero b
//   4. 2^21 LFSR-driven broad pairs
// Structured operands: both signs x all 32 exponents x 64 mantissas
// (8 corners + 56 LFSR-derived). The retired file-driven testbench is kept at
// tb_vectors.sv (+ vectors.dat) for cross-checking; it is not compiled.
module tb;
  logic [15:0] a, b, y, y_ref;
  int errors;
  longint total;

  fp_mul_e5f10 dut (.a(a), .b(b), .y(y));
  fp_mul_e5f10_ref refm (.a(a), .b(b), .y(y_ref));

  function automatic [15:0] lfsr_next(input [15:0] s);
    lfsr_next = (s >> 1) ^ (s[0] ? 16'hB400 : 16'h0000);
  endfunction

  logic [15:0] structured [0:4095];
  localparam logic [9:0] MANT_CORNERS [0:7] =
      '{10'h000, 10'h001, 10'h002, 10'h003, 10'h3FF, 10'h3FE, 10'h2AA, 10'h155};

  task automatic check();
    #1;
    total = total + 1;
    if (y !== y_ref) begin
      if (errors < 10)
        $display("TB_ERROR a=%0d b=%0d expected_y=%0d actual_y=%0d", a, b, y_ref, y);
      errors = errors + 1;
    end
  endtask

  logic [15:0] lfsr, ra, rb;
  logic [9:0] mant;
  int idx;

  initial begin
    errors = 0; total = 0;

    lfsr = 16'hACE1;
    idx = 0;
    for (int s = 0; s < 2; s++)
      for (int e = 0; e < 32; e++)
        for (int m = 0; m < 64; m++) begin
          if (m < 8) mant = MANT_CORNERS[m];
          else begin lfsr = lfsr_next(lfsr); mant = lfsr[9:0]; end
          structured[idx] = {s[0], e[4:0], mant};
          idx = idx + 1;
        end

    a = 16'h0003; b = 16'h6801; check();
    if (y_ref !== 16'h0E02) begin errors++; $display("TB_ERROR golden self-check 1"); end
    a = 16'h8003; b = 16'h6479; check();
    if (y_ref !== 16'h8AB6) begin errors++; $display("TB_ERROR golden self-check 2"); end
    a = 16'h7521; b = 16'h0003; check();
    if (y_ref !== 16'h1BB2) begin errors++; $display("TB_ERROR golden self-check 3"); end

    for (int sa = 0; sa < 2048; sa++) begin
      a = {sa[10], 5'b00000, sa[9:0]};
      for (int i = 0; i < 4096; i++) begin b = structured[i]; check(); end
    end
    for (int i = 0; i < 4096; i++) begin
      a = structured[i];
      for (int sb = 0; sb < 2048; sb++) begin b = {sb[10], 5'b00000, sb[9:0]}; check(); end
    end
    ra = 16'h0001; rb = 16'hBEEF;
    for (int k = 0; k < (1 << 21); k++) begin
      ra = lfsr_next(ra); rb = lfsr_next(lfsr_next(rb));
      a = ra; b = rb; check();
    end

    $display("TB_SUMMARY total=%0d errors=%0d", total, errors);
    if (errors != 0) $fatal(1, "FAIL");
    $display("PASS");
    $finish;
  end
endmodule

  -> create_file(filename='design_v2.v', content="// fp_mul_e5f10 -- optimized IEEE binary16 multiplier (1+5+10)\nmodule fp_mul_e5f10 (a, b, y);\n  input  [15:0] a;\n  input  [15:0] b;\n  output [15:0] y;\n\n  localparam BIAS = 15;\n  localparam PROD_W = 22;\n\n  // unpack\n  wire       s_a = a[15];\n  wire       s_b = b[15];\n  wire [4:0] e_a = a[14:10];\n  wire [4:0] e_b = b[14:10];\n  wire [9:0] f_a = a[9:0];\n  wire [9:0] f_b = b[9:0];\n\n  // classify\n  wire e_a_zero = (e_a == 5'd0);\n  wire e_b_zero = (e_b == 5'd0);\n  wire e_a_ones = (e_a == 5'd31);\n  wire e_b_ones = (e_b == 5'd31);\n  wire zero_a   = e_a_zero & (f_a == 10'd0);\n  wire zero_b   = e_b_zero & (f_b == 10'd0);\n  wire nan_a    = e_a_ones & (f_a != 10'd0);\n  wire nan_b    = e_b_ones & (f_b != 10'd0);\n  wire inf_a    = e_a_ones & (f_a == 10'd0);\n  wire inf_b    = e_b_ones & (f_b == 10'd0);\n\n  wire nan_in  = nan_a | nan_b | (inf_a & zero_b) | (zero_a & inf_b);\n  wire inf_in  = inf_a | inf_b;\n  wire zero_in = zero_a | zero_b;\n  wire s_y     = s_a ^ s_b;\n\n  // effective significands and exponents\n  wire [10:0] m_a    = {~e_a_zero, f_a};\n  wire [10:0] m_b    = {~e_b_zero, f_b};\n  wire [4:0]  ea_eff = e_a_zero ? 5'd1 : e_a;\n  wire [4:0]  eb_eff = e_b_zero ? 5'd1 : e_b;\n\n  // significand product\n  wire [PROD_W-1:0] prod = m_a * m_b;\n\n  // leading-zero count via priority encoder (structured, tree-like)\n  wire [21:0] not_prod = ~prod;\n  // OR-reduction for groups\n  wire [3:0] g0; (prod >> total_shift) : {PROD_W{1'b0}};\n  end\n  wire [9:0]  frac_trunc = sub_shifted[9:0];\n  wire        guard_s    = bit_at(prod,  total_shift - 1);\n  wire        sticky_s   = bit_at(below, total_shift - 2);\n  wire        round_s    = guard_s & (sticky_s | frac_trunc[0]);\n  wire [10:0] frac_sum   = {1'b0, frac_trunc} + {10'd0, round_s};\n  wire        carry_s    = frac_sum[10];\n  wire [9:0]  frac_sub   = frac_sum[9:0];\n  wire [4:0]  exp_sub    = {4'd0, carry_s};\n\n  // pack\n  wire is_nan      = nan_in;\n  wire is_inf      = ~is_nan & (inf_in | (overflow & ~zero_in));\n  wire is_sub      = ~is_nan & ~is_inf & underflow;\n  wire sub_is_zero = is_sub & (exp_sub == 5'd0) & (frac_sub == 10'd0);\n  wire is_zero     = ~is_nan & ~is_inf & (zero_in | sub_is_zero);\n\n  wire [4:0] exp_y  = (is_nan | is_inf) ? 5'd31 : is_zero ? 5'd0 : is_sub ? exp_sub : exp_norm;\n  wire [9:0] frac_y = is_nan ? 10'h200 : (is_inf | is_zero) ? 10'd0 : is_sub ? frac_sub : frac_norm;\n  wire       sign_y = is_nan ? 1'b0 : s_y;\n\n  assign y = {sign_y, exp_y, frac_y};\nendmodule\n")

Wait, but `21 - lead_grp4` when lead_grp4 ranges 0-3 gives 21,20,19,18 — that's correct since group 3 covers bits 18-21. For group 0 (bits 0-5), `21 - lead_grp6` gives 21,20,...,16 — but bit 0 being highest should give lz=0, not 21! The issue is that `21 - lead_grp6` for group 0 (bits 5..0) is wrong. I see the fundamental error: the subtraction base depends on the group. Group 0 (bits 5..0): highest bit is 5, so `lz = 5 - lead_grp6`. Group 1 (bits 11..6): `lz = 11 - lead_grp6`. Group 2 (bits 17..12): `lz = 17 - lead_grp6`. Group 3 (bits 21..18): `lz = 21 - lead_grp4`.

But wait — group 0 has highest bit 5, but when group 0 is the ONLY group with set bits, `g1` would be `2'b00` (no higher groups have set bits) — but if group 0 has set bits AND group 1 also has set bits, g1 wouldn't be 00. The issue is that my priority logic checks groups from highest to lowest but the `g1` encoding is wrong. Let me rethink this.

[ASSISTANT]
That was a mess. Let me think carefully and write a clean version.

The original LZ loop: `lz` starts at 0, and for each `i` from 0 to 21, if `prod[i]` is set, `lz = 21 - i`. The loop goes from i=0 to i=21, so the LAST assignment wins — that's the one with the highest `i`. So `lz = 21 - highest_set_bit`. If no bit is set, `lz = 0`.

A `casex` from MSB to LSB gives priority to the highest set bit:
- `prod[21]=1` → lz = 21-21 = 0
- `prod[20]=1` → lz = 21-20 = 1
- ...
- `prod[0]=1` → lz = 21-0 = 21
- all zero → lz = 0 (default)

This matches! Let me write it correctly.

(prod >> total_shift) : {PROD_W{1'b0}};\n  end\n  wire [9:0]  frac_trunc = sub_shifted[9:0];\n  wire        guard_s    = bit_at(prod,  total_shift - 1);\n  wire        sticky_s   = bit_at(below, total_shift - 2);\n  wire        round_s    = guard_s & (sticky_s | frac_trunc[0]);\n  wire [10:0] frac_sum   = {1'b0, frac_trunc} + {10'd0, round_s};\n  wire        carry_s    = frac_sum[10];\n  wire [9:0]  frac_sub   = frac_sum[9:0];\n  wire [4:0]  exp_sub    = {4'd0, carry_s};\n\n  // 8. pack\n  wire is_nan      = nan_in;\n  wire is_inf      = ~is_nan & (inf_in | (overflow & ~zero_in));\n  wire is_sub      = ~is_nan & ~is_inf & underflow;\n  wire sub_is_zero = is_sub & (exp_sub == 5'd0) & (frac_sub == 10'd0);\n  wire is_zero     = ~is_nan & ~is_inf & (zero_in | sub_is_zero);\n\n  wire [4:0] exp_y  = (is_nan | is_inf) ? 5'd31 : is_zero ? 5'd0 : is_sub ? exp_sub : exp_norm;\n  wire [9:0] frac_y = is_nan ? 10'h200 : (is_inf | is_zero) ? 10'd0 : is_sub ? frac_sub : frac_norm;\n  wire       sign_y = is_nan ? 1'b0 : s_y;\n\n  assign y = {sign_y, exp_y, frac_y};\nendmodule\n", filename='design_v4.v')

[ASSISTANT]
Correctness passes and cost is 144,463 (slightly better than baseline 145,429). The delay improved from 1338 to 1308, but area went up slightly. The critical path now goes to `y[6]` (exponent bit).

Now let me try more aggressive optimizations. The main cost is delay × area. Let me try a different approach — simplify the below tree (only compute the bits actually needed for sticky), and try to optimize the subnormal path.

Key observations for optimization:
1. The `below` tree computes 22 OR reductions, but only a few are used as sticky bits
2. The `bit_at` function creates a variable-indexed lookup which is essentially a barrel shifter
3. The two separate paths (normal + subnormal) duplicate a lot of logic

Let me try to simplify the `below` tree and the `bit_at` calls. For the normal path, `sr` ranges from 0 to 11 (since need_right means lz ≤ 11). The sticky bit needs `below[sr-2]` where sr ranges 0..11. For the subnormal path, `total_shift` ranges from about -6 to 26.

Let me try a different approach: precompute a wider set of guard/sticky bits using the barrel-shifted `shifted` and `sub_shifted` values, rather than the `bit_at` function.
  -> create_file(content="// fp_mul_e5f10 -- v5: simplified sticky/guard extraction\nmodule fp_mul_e5f10 (a, b, y);\n  input  [15:0] a;\n  input  [15:0] b;\n  output [15:0] y;\n\n  localparam BIAS         = 15;\n  localparam MAX_E        = 31;\n  localparam MAX_FINITE_E = 30;\n  localparam PROD_W       = 22;\n\n  wire       s_a = a[15];\n  wire       s_b = b[15];\n  wire [4:0] e_a = a[14:10];\n  wire [4:0] e_b = b[14:10];\n  wire [9:0] f_a = a[9:0];\n  wire [9:0] f_b = b[9:0];\n\n  wire e_a_zero = (e_a == 5'd0);\n  wire e_b_zero = (e_b == 5'd0);\n  wire e_a_ones = (e_a == MAX_E);\n  wire e_b_ones = (e_b == MAX_E);\n  wire zero_a   = e_a_zero & (f_a == 10'd0);\n  wire zero_b   = e_b_zero & (f_b == 10'd0);\n  wire nan_a    = e_a_ones & (f_a != 10'd0);\n  wire nan_b    = e_b_ones & (f_b != 10'd0);\n  wire inf_a    = e_a_ones & (f_a == 10'd0);\n  wire inf_b    = e_b_ones & (f_b == 10'd0);\n\n  wire nan_in  = nan_a | nan_b | (inf_a & zero_b) | (zero_a & inf_b);\n  wire inf_in  = inf_a | inf_b;\n  wire zero_in = zero_a | zero_b;\n  wire s_y     = s_a ^ s_b;\n\n  wire [10:0] m_a    = {~e_a_zero, f_a};\n  wire [10:0] m_b    = {~e_b_zero, f_b};\n  wire [4:0]  ea_eff = e_a_zero ? 5'd1 : e_a;\n  wire [4:0]  eb_eff = e_b_zero ? 5'd1 : e_b;\n\n  wire [PROD_W-1:0] prod = m_a * m_b;\n\n  // leading-zero count: tree-based priority encoder\n  wire prod_nz = |prod;\n  wire hi_nz   = |prod[21:11];\n  wire lo_nz   = |prod[10:0];\n\n  function [3:0] enc11;\n    input [10:0] v;\n    begin\n      if      (v[10]) enc11 = 4'd0;\n      else if (v[9])  enc11 = 4'd1;\n      else if (v[8])  enc11 = 4'd2;\n      else if (v[7])  enc11 = 4'd3;\n      else if (v[6])  enc11 = 4'd4;\n      else if (v[5])  enc11 = 4'd5;\n      else if (v[4])  enc11 = 4'd6;\n      else if (v[3])  enc11 = 4'd7;\n      else if (v[2])  enc11 = 4'd8;\n      else if (v[1])  enc11 = 4'd9;\n      else            enc11 = 4'd10;\n    end\n  endfunction\n\n  wire [4:0] lz_hi = {1'b0, enc11(prod[21:11])};\n  wire [4:0] lz_lo = {1'b0, enc11(prod[10:0])} + 5'd11;\n  wire [4:0] lz = ~prod_nz ? 5'd0 : hi_nz ? lz_hi : lz_lo;\n\n  // OR-below tree (for sticky bits)\n  wire [PROD_W-1:0] below;\n  genvar k;\n  generate\n    for (k = 0; k < PROD_W; k = k + 1) begin : g_below\n      assign below[k] = |prod[k:0];\n    end\n  endgenerate\n\n  function bit_at;\n    input [PROD_W-1:0] v;\n    input integer      idx;\n    begin\n      bit_at = (idx >= 0 && idx < PROD_W) ? v[idx] : 1'b0;\n    end\n  endfunction\n\n  // normalise and round a normal result\n  wire              need_right = (lz <= 5'd11);\n  wire [4:0]        sr         = 5'd11 - lz;\n  wire [4:0]        sl         = lz - 5'd11;\n  wire [PROD_W-1:0] shifted    = need_right ? (prod >> sr) : (prod << sl);\n  wire [10:0]       mant_pre   = shifted[10:0];\n\n  wire guard    = need_right ? bit_at(prod,  sr - 1) : 1'b0;\n  wire sticky   = need_right ? bit_at(below, sr - 2) : 1'b0;\n  wire round_up = guard & (sticky | mant_pre[0]);\n\n  wire [11:0] mant_round = {1'b0, mant_pre} + {11'd0, round_up};\n  wire        carry      = mant_round[11];\n  wire [10:0] mant_post  = carry ? mant_round[11:1] : mant_round[10:0];\n  wire [9:0]  frac_norm  = mant_post[9:0];\n\n  // exponent of a normal result\n  wire [7:0] exp_sum     = {3'd0, ea_eff} + {3'd0, eb_eff};\n  wire [7:0] lhs         = exp_sum + 8'd1 + {7'd0, carry};\n  wire [7:0] limit_under = BIAS + {3'd0, lz};\n  wire [7:0] limit_over  = BIAS + MAX_FINITE_E + {3'd0, lz};\n  wire       underflow   = (lhs <= limit_under);\n  wire       overflow    = (lhs >  limit_over);\n  wire [7:0] e_norm      = lhs - limit_under;\n  wire [4:0] exp_norm    = e_norm[4:0];\n\n  // subnormal result\n  integer           total_shift;\n  reg [PROD_W-1:0]  sub_shifted;\n  always @* begin\n    total_shift = (BIAS + 10 + 1) - $signed({1'b0, exp_sum});\n    sub_shifted = (total_shift >= 0 && total_shift < PROD_W) ? (prod >> total_shift) : {PROD_W{1'b0}};\n  end\n  wire [9:0]  frac_trunc = sub_shifted[9:0];\n  wire        guard_s    = bit_at(prod,  total_shift - 1);\n  wire        sticky_s   = bit_at(below, total_shift - 2);\n  wire        round_s    = guard_s & (sticky_s | frac_trunc[0]);\n  wire [10:0] frac_sum   = {1'b0, frac_trunc} + {10'd0, round_s};\n  wire        carry_s    = frac_sum[10];\n  wire [9:0]  frac_sub   = frac_sum[9:0];\n  wire [4:0]  exp_sub    = {4'd0, carry_s};\n\n  // pack\n  wire is_nan      = nan_in;\n  wire is_inf      = ~is_nan & (inf_in | (overflow & ~zero_in));\n  wire is_sub      = ~is_nan & ~is_inf & underflow;\n  wire sub_is_zero = is_sub & (exp_sub == 5'd0) & (frac_sub == 10'd0);\n  wire is_zero     = ~is_nan & ~is_inf & (zero_in | sub_is_zero);\n\n  wire [4:0] exp_y  = (is_nan | is_inf) ? 5'd31 : is_zero ? 5'd0 : is_sub ? exp_sub : exp_norm;\n  wire [9:0] frac_y = is_nan ? 10'h200 : (is_inf | is_zero) ? 10'd0 : is_sub ? frac_sub : frac_norm;\n  wire       sign_y = is_nan ? 1'b0 : s_y;\n\n  assign y = {sign_y, exp_y, frac_y};\nendmodule\n", filename='design_v5.v')

[ASSISTANT]
The target_delay doesn't affect the synthesis output — Yosys always produces the same netlist regardless. The cost is area × achieved_delay, and the achieved delay is fixed at 1308 for this design structure.

Let me now focus on structural changes. The critical path goes through:
1. LZ computation (from prod)
2. Shift (sr/sl computation from lz)
3. Barrel shift (prod >> sr)
4. Rounding (guard, sticky, round_up)
5. Add (mant_round)
6. Exponent computation (exp_sum, lhs, etc.)
7. Packing (mux for final output)

The path goes a[10] → ... → y[6], so it starts from an input and goes all the way through. Let me try to shorten the critical path by simplifying the logic. 

Key idea: The `bit_at` function with variable index is expensive. Instead of using `bit_at(prod, sr-1)` and `bit_at(below, sr-2)`, I can compute guard and sticky from the barrel-shifted result directly. When `need_right` (right shift), the shifted value has the mantissa in bits [10:0], and the guard is bit 11 of the shifted value, sticky is bits below that.

Let me try this approach:
  -> create_file(content="// fp_mul_e5f10 -- v6: extract guard/sticky from barrel shift\nmodule fp_mul_e5f10 (a, b, y);\n  input  [15:0] a;\n  input  [15:0] b;\n  output [15:0] y;\n\n  localparam BIAS         = 15;\n  localparam MAX_E        = 31;\n  localparam MAX_FINITE_E = 30;\n  localparam PROD_W       = 22;\n\n  wire       s_a = a[15];\n  wire       s_b = b[15];\n  wire [4:0] e_a = a[14:10];\n  wire [4:0] e_b = b[14:10];\n  wire [9:0] f_a = a[9:0];\n  wire [9:0] f_b = b[9:0];\n\n  wire e_a_zero = (e_a == 5'd0);\n  wire e_b_zero = (e_b == 5'd0);\n  wire e_a_ones = (e_a == MAX_E);\n  wire e_b_ones = (e_b == MAX_E);\n  wire zero_a   = e_a_zero & (f_a == 10'd0);\n  wire zero_b   = e_b_zero & (f_b == 10'd0);\n  wire nan_a    = e_a_ones & (f_a != 10'd0);\n  wire nan_b    = e_b_ones & (f_b != 10'd0);\n  wire inf_a    = e_a_ones & (f_a == 10'd0);\n  wire inf_b    = e_b_ones & (f_b == 10'd0);\n\n  wire nan_in  = nan_a | nan_b | (inf_a & zero_b) | (zero_a & inf_b);\n  wire inf_in  = inf_a | inf_b;\n  wire zero_in = zero_a | zero_b;\n  wire s_y     = s_a ^ s_b;\n\n  wire [10:0] m_a    = {~e_a_zero, f_a};\n  wire [10:0] m_b    = {~e_b_zero, f_b};\n  wire [4:0]  ea_eff = e_a_zero ? 5'd1 : e_a;\n  wire [4:0]  eb_eff = e_b_zero ? 5'd1 : e_b;\n\n  wire [PROD_W-1:0] prod = m_a * m_b;\n\n  // leading-zero count: tree-based priority encoder\n  wire prod_nz = |prod;\n  wire hi_nz   = |prod[21:11];\n  wire lo_nz   = |prod[10:0];\n\n  function [3:0] enc11;\n    input [10:0] v;\n    begin\n      if      (v[10]) enc11 = 4'd0;\n      else if (v[9])  enc11 = 4'd1;\n      else if (v[8])  enc11 = 4'd2;\n      else if (v[7])  enc11 = 4'd3;\n      else if (v[6])  enc11 = 4'd4;\n      else if (v[5])  enc11 = 4'd5;\n      else if (v[4])  enc11 = 4'd6;\n      else if (v[3])  enc11 = 4'd7;\n      else if (v[2])  enc11 = 4'd8;\n      else if (v[1])  enc11 = 4'd9;\n      else            enc11 = 4'd10;\n    end\n  endfunction\n\n  wire [4:0] lz_hi = {1'b0, enc11(prod[21:11])};\n  wire [4:0] lz_lo = {1'b0, enc11(prod[10:0])} + 5'd11;\n  wire [4:0] lz = ~prod_nz ? 5'd0 : hi_nz ? lz_hi : lz_lo;\n\n  // OR-below tree (for sticky bits)\n  wire [PROD_W-1:0] below;\n  genvar k;\n  generate\n    for (k = 0; k < PROD_W; k = k + 1) begin : g_below\n      assign below[k] = |prod[k:0];\n    end\n  endgenerate\n\n  function bit_at;\n    input [PROD_W-1:0] v;\n    input integer      idx;\n    begin\n      bit_at = (idx >= 0 && idx < PROD_W) ? v[idx] : 1'b0;\n    end\n  endfunction\n\n  // Normal path: normalize and round\n  wire              need_right = (lz <= 5'd11);\n  wire [4:0]        sr         = 5'd11 - lz;\n  wire [4:0]        sl         = lz - 5'd11;\n  wire [PROD_W-1:0] shifted    = need_right ? (prod >> sr) : (prod << sl);\n  wire [10:0]       mant_pre   = shifted[10:0];\n\n  // Guard and sticky from barrel-shifted result\n  // When need_right: shifted = prod >> sr, guard = shifted[11], sticky = |shifted[21:12]\n  // When !need_right (left shift): no bits shifted out, guard=sticky=0\n  wire guard    = need_right ? shifted[11] : 1'b0;\n  wire sticky   = need_right ? (|shifted[21:12]) : 1'b0;\n  wire round_up = guard & (sticky | mant_pre[0]);\n\n  wire [11:0] mant_round = {1'b0, mant_pre} + {11'd0, round_up};\n  wire        carry      = mant_round[11];\n  wire [10:0] mant_post  = carry ? mant_round[11:1] : mant_round[10:0];\n  wire [9:0]  frac_norm  = mant_post[9:0];\n\n  // exponent of a normal result\n  wire [7:0] exp_sum     = {3'd0, ea_eff} + {3'd0, eb_eff};\n  wire [7:0] lhs         = exp_sum + 8'd1 + {7'd0, carry};\n  wire [7:0] limit_under = BIAS + {3'd0, lz};\n  wire [7:0] limit_over  = BIAS + MAX_FINITE_E + {3'd0, lz};\n  wire       underflow   = (lhs <= limit_under);\n  wire       overflow    = (lhs >  limit_over);\n  wire [7:0] e_norm      = lhs - limit_under;\n  wire [4:0] exp_norm    = e_norm[4:0];\n\n  // subnormal result\n  integer           total_shift;\n  reg [PROD_W-1:0]  sub_shifted;\n  always @* begin\n    total_shift = (BIAS + 10 + 1) - $signed({1'b0, exp_sum});\n    sub_shifted = (total_shift >= 0 && total_shift < PROD_W) ? (prod >> total_shift) : {PROD_W{1'b0}};\n  end\n  wire [9:0]  frac_trunc = sub_shifted[9:0];\n  wire        guard_s    = bit_at(prod,  total_shift - 1);\n  wire        sticky_s   = bit_at(below, total_shift - 2);\n  wire        round_s    = guard_s & (sticky_s | frac_trunc[0]);\n  wire [10:0] frac_sum   = {1'b0, frac_trunc} + {10'd0, round_s};\n  wire        carry_s    = frac_sum[10];\n  wire [9:0]  frac_sub   = frac_sum[9:0];\n  wire [4:0]  exp_sub    = {4'd0, carry_s};\n\n  // pack\n  wire is_nan      = nan_in;\n  wire is_inf      = ~is_nan & (inf_in | (overflow & ~zero_in));\n  wire is_sub      = ~is_nan & ~is_inf & underflow;\n  wire sub_is_zero = is_sub & (exp_sub == 5'd0) & (frac_sub == 10'd0);\n  wire is_zero     = ~is_nan & ~is_inf & (zero_in | sub_is_zero);\n\n  wire [4:0] exp_y  = (is_nan | is_inf) ? 5'd31 : is_zero ? 5'd0 : is_sub ? exp_sub : exp_norm;\n  wire [9:0] frac_y = is_nan ? 10'h200 : (is_inf | is_zero) ? 10'd0 : is_sub ? frac_sub : frac_norm;\n  wire       sign_y = is_nan ? 1'b0 : s_y;\n\n  assign y = {sign_y, exp_y, frac_y};\nendmodule\n", filename='design_v6.v')

Wait, that doesn't make sense either. Let me think about the normalization direction. The product has bits [21:0]. The leading 1 is at bit `21-lz`. For a normal result, we want the leading 1 at bit 10 (the implicit 1 position for a 10-bit fraction with 11-bit significand).

If lz ≤ 11 (need_right): the leading 1 is at bit `21-lz ≥ 10`. We shift RIGHT by `sr = 11-lz`. After shifting, the leading 1 moves from bit `21-lz` to bit `21-lz-sr = 21-lz-(11-lz) = 10`. Good. The key observation: in the normal path, the shift is `sr = 11 - lz` (right shift to put leading 1 at bit 10). In the subnormal path, the shift is `total_shift = 26 - exp_sum`. The subnormal shift is LARGER than the normal shift (it shifts more to the right, producing a subnormal result).

What if I always compute the subnormal shift, and if the result overflows the subnormal range (carry_s = 1), use the normal path? This way, the subnormal path handles both normal and subnormal results, and the normal path is only for the overflow case.

[ASSISTANT]
The precompute approach actually made it worse (149,796 vs 144,463) — the extra area for both carry paths outweighs the delay benefit. Let me go back to v4 and try a different approach.

Let me try to simplify the `below` tree by only computing the bits actually needed. For the normal path, `sr` ranges 0..11, so `below[sr-2]` uses below[0]..below[9]. For the subnormal path, `total_shift` ranges 0..24 (when exp_sum ranges 2..26), so `below[total_shift-2]` uses below[0]..below[22]. So the full tree is needed for the subnormal path.

But wait — the subnormal path is only used when `underflow` is true, which means `lhs <= limit_under`. The subnormal result is only computed when the product is very small. In those cases, `exp_sum` is small (≤ about 15 + lz), so `total_shift` is large. Maybe I can limit the range.

Actually, let me try a completely different optimization: combine the normal and subnormal paths. Instead of computing both and muxing, compute a single shift that handles both cases. 

Let me think about the math:
- Normal: shift = 11 - lz (right if lz < 11, left if lz > 11), result has leading 1 at bit 10
- Subnormal: shift = 26 - exp_sum (always right shift), result is in subnormal range

The subnormal shift = 26 - exp_sum. The normal shift = 11 - lz. The relationship: in the normal case, the final exponent is `exp_sum + 1 + carry - (15 + lz)`. When this is ≤ 0 (subnormal), the subnormal path uses shift = 26 - exp_sum.

If the product is in the normal range (not subnormal), we use shift = 11 - lz. If in the subnormal range, we use shift = 26 - exp_sum. These are different computations.

What if I always use the subnormal shift and check if the result overflows to normal? The subnormal shift `total_shift = 26 - exp_sum` is independent of lz (the product's leading zeros). When we shift the product right by `total_shift`, we get the subnormal fraction. If this fraction has a carry (rounds up to the smallest normal), then exp=1 and we have the smallest normal.

But for normal-range results, the subnormal shift would shift too much and lose all the mantissa bits. So I can't use a single shift.

OK so v4 (144,463) is still the best, better than starting_point (145,429). The enc11-based LZ is slightly better than the loop-based one.

Let me now try to optimize the `below` tree by making it a tree structure (balanced OR-reduction) instead of a ripple chain. The current `below[k] = |prod[k:0]` creates a linear chain. A tree structure might have shorter delay.

But wait — the `below` tree is used for sticky bits, and the critical path doesn't seem to go through it (the critical path goes through the LZ → shift → round → exponent → mux). So optimizing the `below` tree might not help the critical path, but it could reduce area.

Let me try a different approach: eliminate the `bit_at` function for the normal path by extracting guard and sticky from the barrel-shifted product. I need to think about this differently.

When `need_right` (lz ≤ 11, sr = 11 - lz, shift right by sr):
- `shifted = prod >> sr`
- `mant_pre = shifted[10:0]` (bits [sr+10:sr] of prod)
- Guard = prod[sr-1] = the bit just below the mantissa
- Sticky = |prod[sr-2:0] = below[sr-2]

If I compute `shifted_ext = {1'b0, prod} >> sr` (23-bit), then:
- mant_pre = shifted_ext[10:0] (same as before)
- The bits below mant in shifted_ext are shifted_ext[11], shifted_ext[12], etc. But these are prod[sr+11], prod[sr+12], etc. — which are ABOVE the mantissa, not below!

The issue is fundamental: the guard/sticky bits are the bits that are shifted OUT (below the mantissa in the original product), not the bits that remain above the mantissa. The barrel-shifted result only contains the kept bits, not the lost ones.

To extract the lost bits, I need a separate barrel shift or OR-tree. The `below` tree IS the right approach.

Let me try yet another approach: instead of the `below` tree, compute the sticky bit using a reversed barrel shift. `sticky = |(prod & ((1 << (sr-1)) - 1))` — OR all bits [sr-2:0]. But this is what `below[sr-2]` does. For the subnormal path, it's `below[total_shift-2]`.

145,234 — worse than v4 (144,463). The simplified classification didn't help; it actually changed the synthesis result slightly for the worse.

Let me try a more aggressive approach. I'll try to merge the two barrel shifters (normal `shifted` and subnormal `sub_shifted`) into one. The normal shift amount is `11 - lz` (signed, can be negative for left shift), and the subnormal shift amount is `26 - exp_sum` (always right, larger). 

The key idea: when the result is subnormal, the subnormal shift is used; otherwise, the normal shift is used. If I compute a single "unified shift" that's the maximum of the two, I might be able to share the barrel shifter. But the shift directions differ (normal can be left, subnormal is always right).

Actually, let me try a different approach: compute the subnormal path using the normal path's barrel-shifted result. The normal shift puts the leading 1 at bit 10. The subnormal shift puts the product at a subnormal position. The relationship: `sub_shift = total_shift = 26 - exp_sum`, and `normal_shift = 11 - lz`. The difference is `sub_shift - normal_shift = 26 - exp_sum - 11 + lz = 15 + lz - exp_sum`. When the result is subnormal, `lhs ≤ limit_under`, i.e., `exp_sum + 1 + carry ≤ 15 + lz`, i.e., `exp_sum ≤ 14 + lz` (ignoring carry). So `15 + lz - exp_sum ≥ 1`. The subnormal shift is larger.

So I could: do the normal shift first, then shift additionally by `sub_shift - normal_shift` to get the subnormal position. But this creates a cascade of two shifts, which is worse.

Let me try to reduce the area by eliminating the `below` tree and computing the sticky bit from a single wide OR of the lower bits of the barrel-shifted result.

[ASSISTANT]
This is the same as v4. Let me try a genuinely different approach. Let me eliminate the `below` tree and `bit_at` by using a barrel shift that keeps the guard and sticky bits.

The key idea: I'll pad the product with extra bits on the right, then barrel-shift. The shifted result will have the mantissa, guard, and sticky bits at fixed positions.

For the normal path: I need to shift the product so the leading 1 lands at bit 10. The shift is `sr = 11 - lz` (right) or `sl = lz - 11` (left). If I extend the product: `{prod, 2'd0}` (24 bits) and shift right by `sr`, then:
- mant_pre = bits [12:2] of the shifted result (11 bits, = bits [sr+12:sr+2] of extended prod)
- Wait, this doesn't help because I'm still losing the guard/sticky bits.

Let me think differently. The guard/sticky bits are the bits shifted out below the mantissa. If I pad the product with zeros on the right and shift right, the padded zeros go into the low bits, and the "lost" bits from the product go into... they're still lost because the barrel shifter discards them.

The only way to keep the guard/sticky is to capture them separately. The `below` tree is the standard way to do this.

Let me try a completely different approach: use the original `starting_point.v` (with the loop-based LZ) but try to reduce the area by simplifying the final mux logic.

For the subnormal path, the guard bit is at `prod[total_shift-1]` and sticky is `|prod[0:total_shift-2]`. Instead of using the `below` tree, I could compute this as `|(prod & mask)` where mask has bits [total_shift-2:0] set. But this requires a variable mask, which is equivalent.

Let me try another idea: compute the guard and sticky for BOTH paths using a single barrel shift with extra output bits. I'll use a 24-bit barrel shift `{prod, 2'b00}` and extract:
- mantissa from bits [12:2]
- guard from bit [13]  
- sticky from bit [14] (OR-reduced from bits above)

No wait, this still doesn't work because the guard/sticky bits are BELOW the mantissa, not above.

Let me try yet another approach: instead of `bit_at(prod, sr-1)` and `bit_at(below, sr-2)`, compute a 2-bit "round_info" using a barrel shift of a reversed product. 

Actually, let me try something completely different: use a lookup-table approach for the guard/sticky. Since `sr` only ranges 0-11, I can use a 12:1 mux.
  -> create_file(content="// fp_mul_e5f10 -- v15: explicit mux for guard/sticky, loop-based LZ\nmodule fp_mul_e5f10 (a, b, y);\n  input  [15:0] a;\n  input  [15:0] b;\n  output [15:0] y;\n\n  localparam BIAS = 15;\n  localparam MAX_E = 31;\n  localparam MAX_FINITE_E = 30;\n  localparam PROD_W = 22;\n\n  wire       s_a = a[15];\n  wire       s_b = b[15];\n  wire [4:0] e_a = a[14:10];\n  wire [4:0] e_b = b[14:10];\n  wire [9:0] f_a = a[9:0];\n  wire [9:0] f_b = b[9:0];\n\n  wire e_a_zero = (e_a == 5'd0);\n  wire e_b_zero = (e_b == 5'd0);\n  wire e_a_ones = (e_a == MAX_E);\n  wire e_b_ones = (e_b == MAX_E);\n  wire zero_a   = e_a_zero & (f_a == 10'd0);\n  wire zero_b   = e_b_zero & (f_b == 10'd0);\n  wire nan_a    = e_a_ones & (f_a != 10'd0);\n  wire nan_b    = e_b_ones & (f_b != 10'd0);\n  wire inf_a    = e_a_ones & (f_a == 10'd0);\n  wire inf_b    = e_b_ones & (f_b == 10'd0);\n\n  wire nan_in  = nan_a | nan_b | (inf_a & zero_b) | (zero_a & inf_b);\n  wire inf_in  = inf_a | inf_b;\n  wire zero_in = zero_a | zero_b;\n  wire s_y     = s_a ^ s_b;\n\n  wire [10:0] m_a    = {~e_a_zero, f_a};\n  wire [10:0] m_b    = {~e_b_zero, f_b};\n  wire [4:0]  ea_eff = e_a_zero ? 5'd1 : e_a;\n  wire [4:0]  eb_eff = e_b_zero ? 5'd1 : e_b;\n\n  wire [PROD_W-1:0] prod = m_a * m_b;\n\n  // leading-zero count (loop-based, same as original)\n  reg [4:0] lz;\n  integer   i;\n  always @* begin\n    lz = 5'd0;\n    for (i = 0; i < PROD_W; i = i + 1)\n      if (prod[i]) lz = 5'd21 - i[4:0];\n  end\n\n  // OR-below tree\n  wire [PROD_W-1:0] below;\n  genvar k;\n  generate\n    for (k = 0; k < PROD_W; k = k + 1) begin : g_below\n      assign below[k] = |prod[k:0];\n    end\n  endgenerate\n\n  function bit_at;\n    input [PROD_W-1:0] v;\n    input integer      idx;\n    begin\n      bit_at = (idx >= 0 && idx < PROD_W) ? v[idx] : 1'b0;\n    end\n  endfunction\n\n  // Normal path\n  wire              need_right = (lz <= 5'd11);\n  wire [4:0]        sr         = 5'd11 - lz;\n  wire [4:0]        sl         = lz - 5'd11;\n  wire [PROD_W-1:0] shifted    = need_right ? (prod >> sr) : (prod << sl);\n  wire [10:0]       mant_pre   = shifted[10:0];\n\n  wire guard    = need_right ? bit_at(prod,  sr - 1) : 1'b0;\n  wire sticky   = need_right ? bit_at(below, sr - 2) : 1'b0;\n  wire round_up = guard & (sticky | mant_pre[0]);\n\n  wire [11:0] mant_round = {1'b0, mant_pre} + {11'd0, round_up};\n  wire        carry      = mant_round[11];\n  wire [10:0] mant_post  = carry ? mant_round[11:1] : mant_round[10:0];\n  wire [9:0]  frac_norm  = mant_post[9:0];\n\n  wire [7:0] exp_sum     = {3'd0, ea_eff} + {3'd0, eb_eff};\n  wire [7:0] lhs         = exp_sum + 8'd1 + {7'd0, carry};\n  wire [7:0] limit_under = BIAS + {3'd0, lz};\n  wire [7:0] limit_over  = BIAS + MAX_FINITE_E + {3'd0, lz};\n  wire       underflow   = (lhs <= limit_under);\n  wire       overflow    = (lhs >  limit_over);\n  wire [7:0] e_norm      = lhs - limit_under;\n  wire [4:0] exp_norm    = e_norm[4:0];\n\n  // Subnormal path\n  integer           total_shift;\n  reg [PROD_W-1:0]  sub_shifted;\n  always @* begin\n    total_shift = (BIAS + 10 + 1) - $signed({1'b0, exp_sum});\n    sub_shifted = (total_shift >= 0 && total_shift < PROD_W) ? (prod >> total_shift) : {PROD_W{1'b0}};\n  end\n  wire [9:0]  frac_trunc = sub_shifted[9:0];\n  wire        guard_s    = bit_at(prod,  total_shift - 1);\n  wire        sticky_s   = bit_at(below, total_shift - 2);\n  wire        round_s    = guard_s & (sticky_s | frac_trunc[0]);\n  wire [10:0] frac_sum   = {1'b0, frac_trunc} + {10'd0, round_s};\n  wire        carry_s    = frac_sum[10];\n  wire [9:0]  frac_sub   = frac_sum[9:0];\n  wire [4:0]  exp_sub    = {4'd0, carry_s};\n\n  // pack\n  wire is_nan      = nan_in;\n  wire is_inf      = ~is_nan & (inf_in | (overflow & ~zero_in));\n  wire is_sub      = ~is_nan & ~is_inf & underflow;\n  wire sub_is_zero = is_sub & (exp_sub == 5'd0) & (frac_sub == 10'd0);\n  wire is_zero     = ~is_nan & ~is_inf & (zero_in | sub_is_zero);\n\n  wire [4:0] exp_y  = (is_nan | is_inf) ? 5'd31 : is_zero ? 5'd0 : is_sub ? exp_sub : exp_norm;\n  wire [9:0] frac_y = is_nan ? 10'h200 : (is_inf | is_zero) ? 10'd0 : is_sub ? frac_sub : frac_norm;\n  wire       sign_y = is_nan ? 1'b0 : s_y;\n\n  assign y = {sign_y, exp_y, frac_y};\nendmodule\n", filename='design_v15.v')

[ASSISTANT]
This is just the starting_point with loop-based LZ (same as starting_point.v). Let me try a different approach — eliminate the `below` tree by computing guard and sticky from the barrel-shifted result of a WIDER product. 

The idea: when we right-shift `prod` by `sr`, the bits [sr-1:0] are lost. If I compute `{below[sr-1], below[sr-2]}` — but that's what `bit_at` does.

OK, let me try a completely radical approach: instead of the two-path architecture, use a single path where I compute the product, determine the final exponent, and then shift to the final position (whether normal or subnormal) in one step.

The final position depends on the final exponent `e_final`:
- If `e_final >= 1` (normal): the leading 1 should be at bit 10, shift = lz - 11 (or 11 - lz, depending on direction). Wait, this is what the normal path does.
- If `e_final <= 0` (subnormal): the value is `prod * 2^(e_final - 10)`, shift right by `10 - e_final + lz` ... this is getting complicated.

Let me try something simpler: just try to reduce the delay by using a different `enc11` structure. The current one is an if-else chain (priority encoder). A tree structure might be faster.
  -> edit_file(filename='design_v4.v', new_string="  // enc11: tree-based priority encoder for 11-bit vector\n  // Returns position (0-10) of highest set bit\n  function [3:0] enc11;\n    input [10:0] v;\n    begin\n      if (v[10]) enc11 = 4'd0;\n      else if (v[9]) enc11 = 4'd1;\n      else if (v[8]) enc11 = 4'd2;\n      else if (v[7]) enc11 = 4'd3;\n      else if (v[6]) enc11 = 4'd4;\n      else if (v[5]) enc11 = 4'd5;\n      else if (v[4]) enc11 = 4'd6;\n      else if (v[3]) enc11 = 4'd7;\n      else if (v[2]) enc11 = 4'd8;\n      else if (v[1]) enc11 = 4'd9;\n      else            enc11 = 4'd10;\n    end\n  endfunction\n\n  // Alternative: direct bit extraction for enc (avoid function call overhead)\n  wire [3:0] enc_hi, enc_lo;\n  // For upper half prod[21:11]:\n  assign enc_hi = prod[21] ? 4'd0 : prod[20] ? 4'd1 : prod[19] ? 4'd2 :\n                  prod[18] ? 4'd3 : prod[17] ? 4'd4 : prod[16] ? 4'd5 :\n                  prod[15] ? 4'd6 : prod[14] ? 4'd7 : prod[13] ? 4'd8 :\n                  prod[12] ? 4'd9 : 4'd10;\n  // For lower half prod[10:0]:\n  assign enc_lo = prod[10] ? 4'd0 : prod[9] ? 4'd1 : prod[8] ? 4'd2 :\n                  prod[7] ? 4'd3 : prod[6] ? 4'd4 : prod[5] ? 4'd5 :\n                  prod[4] ? 4'd6 : prod[3] ? 4'd7 : prod[2] ? 4'd8 :\n                  prod[1] ? 4'd9 : 4'd10;", old_string="  function [3:0] enc11;\n    input [10:0] v;\n    begin\n      if      (v[10]) enc11 = 4'd0;\n      else if (v[9])  enc11 = 4'd1;\n      else if (v[8])  enc11 = 4'd2;\n      else if (v[7])  enc11 = 4'd3;\n      else if (v[6])  enc11 = 4'd4;\n      else if (v[5])  enc11 = 4'd5;\n      else if (v[4])  enc11 = 4'd6;\n      else if (v[3])  enc11 = 4'd7;\n      else if (v[2])  enc11 = 4'd8;\n      else if (v[1])  enc11 = 4'd9;\n      else            enc11 = 4'd10;\n    end\n  endfunction")

Please write a brief summary covering:

[ASSISTANT]
## Summary

### 1. Approaches Tried

- **`starting_point.v`** (baseline): Loop-based LZ priority encoder, dual-path (normal + subnormal) with `bit_at` function and `below` OR-tree. Cost: **145,429**.
- **`design_v4.v`** (best): Replaced loop-based LZ with tree-structured priority encoder — split 22-bit product into two 11-bit halves, each encoded by an `enc11` function (if-else chain). Cost: **144,463**.
- **`design_v2.v`**: Attempted custom LZ with group-based encoding — had subtle off-by-one bugs in the lz computation that took multiple iterations to debug, never fully fixed.
- **`design_v6.v`**: Tried extracting guard/sticky from barrel-shifted result — fundamentally wrong because guard/sticky bits are the bits *shifted out* (below mantissa), not extractable from the shifted result.
- **`design_v10.v`**: Precomputed exponent for both carry=0 and carry=1 — increased area more than it reduced delay.
- **`design_v14.v`**: Flat 4-way output mux instead of priority chain — worse delay and area.
- **`design_v11.v`**: Simplified classification (`|f_a` instead of `f_a == 0`) — marginal regression.

### 2. Most Impactful Optimization
The **tree-structured LZ** (splitting 22-bit into two 11-bit halves with separate priority encoders) was the only change that improved cost (~0.7% reduction). It reduced delay from 1338ps to 1308ps while keeping area nearly the same.

### 3. What Didn't Work
- **Guard/sticky from barrel shift output**: The shifted result only contains kept bits; guard/sticky are the lost bits below the mantissa, requiring the `below` OR-tree.
- **Precomputing both carry paths**: Extra area dominated any delay savings.
- **Flat output mux**: Created longer paths through the select logic.
- **Fixed-width `total_shift`**: Variable indexing with 6-bit unsigned created more complex logic than the integer-based approach.
- **`casez`/`casex` for LZ**: Lint failure — X/Z literals not allowed.

### 4. Lessons Learned
- The design is dominated by the 11×11 multiplier and two barrel shifters — hard to optimize further without changing the algorithm fundamentally.
- Unifying the normal/subnormal paths into a single shift+round path is the most promising unexplored direction but requires careful handling of the different shift directions and exponent calculations.
- Small structural changes (LZ encoder) can help, but the synthesis tool is fairly insensitive to RTL micro-optimizations at this level.
- `target_delay` had no effect — Yosys always produced the same netlist regardless of constraint.
